Hello. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate you taking the time. Um... I'm a little embarrassed, to be perfectly honest, and I hope I'm in the right place for this.

Oh, um... well, I suppose it's been a bit more regular than I'd care to admit. I really hope I'm not overstating things, but... since last winter, I think there has hardly been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... little private eating spells.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I wouldn't say I look overweight right now. I'm so sorry if I gave you the wrong impression just now! I know I'm not... you know, heavy. 

It's more that I just really don't see the "too thin" part that they seem to see. When I look in the mirror, I don't see a heavy person, but I definitely don't see a frail one either. I just see... well, just me, I suppose. Just an average woman who needs to keep a very close eye on things so they don't slip. I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that.

And then I just come back down and act as if I'm just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so nobody notices. \n\nIt feels terribly silly of me to be doing this at my age, and I'm so sorry to even bother you with it. Is... is this the right sort of thing to be talking to you about?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating too much and then making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's been a bit more regular than I'd care to admit. I really hope I'm not overstating things, but... since last winter, I think there has hardly been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... little private eating spells. \n\nAnd then, well, I just try to fix it, you know? To make up for it so my weight doesn't get out of hand. I might make myself sick, like I mentioned, or sometimes I just quietly skip my meals the next day, or I'll overdo it a bit with my walking. Just trying to balance the scales, really, so nobody has to worry about me. \n\nDoes... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight when you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... yes, I suppose I am. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly vain or superficial. \n\nIt's just... well, my daughter, and actually a lovely lady from my church, they've both been telling me lately that I look a bit too thin. Which is sweet of them to worry, but... I just don't see it that way at all. If my skirt feels even the slightest bit snug in the morning, or if I step on the scale and it's up just one single pound... I completely panic. It feels as if I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, if that makes any sense? \n\nI just get so frightened of letting myself go. I know I shouldn't let a number on a scale dictate my mood, but I just can't seem to help it. Is... is it normal to worry about it quite that much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look overweight even when others tell you that you're too thin?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I look overweight right now. I'm so sorry if I gave you the wrong impression just now! I know I'm not... you know, heavy. \n\nIt's more that I just really don't see the \"too thin\" part that they seem to see. When I look in the mirror, I don't see a heavy person, but I definitely don't see a frail one either. I just see... well, just me, I suppose. Just an average woman who needs to keep a very close eye on things so they don't slip. I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it properly for you, I just... I really don't see what they see when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I hope that's alright. My weight has actually stayed fairly... well, fairly steady over the past year. \n\nI try very hard to keep it that way, you see. So there haven't been any dramatic jumps up or down, just... maybe a pound or two here and there, which I always try to catch right away before it becomes a problem. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose it can feel a bit difficult to stop sometimes. I really try not to let it get out of hand, I promise I do. \n\nIt's just... once I start, it's almost as if a little switch flips inside me? It's the strangest feeling. It's like I'm just sort of... watching myself keep eating. And there's this part of my brain that's saying, 'Linda, you need to stop now, that's enough,' but... my hands just keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit that. It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is it common for people to struggle with that part?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it properly for you, I just... I really don't see what they see when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I hope that's alright. My weight has actually stayed fairly... well, fairly steady over the past year. \n\nI try very hard to keep it that way, you see. So there haven't been any dramatic jumps up or down, just... maybe a pound or two here and there, which I always try to catch right away before it becomes a problem. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose it can feel a bit difficult to stop sometimes. I really try not to let it get out of hand, I promise I do. \n\nIt's just... once I start, it's almost as if a little switch flips inside me? It's the strangest feeling. It's like I'm just sort of... watching myself keep eating. And there's this part of my brain that's saying, 'Linda, you need to stop now, that's enough,' but... my hands just keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit that. It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I promise I'm completely responsible in every other part of my life! I'm very meticulous at the opticians where I work, and I keep everything else in perfect order. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? I guess what finally made me realize I needed to say something to a doctor is that... sometimes I end up eating a bit too much. Just overdoing it a little, you know? And lately, after that happens, I've been... well, I've been going upstairs and making myself throw up. And then I just come back down and act as if I'm just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so nobody notices. \n\nIt feels terribly silly of me to be doing this at my age, and I'm so sorry to even bother you with it. I really hope I'm not overstating things, but... since last winter, I think there has hardly been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... little private eating spells. \n\nAnd then, well, I just try to fix it, you know? To make up for it so my weight doesn't get out of hand. I might make myself sick, like I mentioned, or sometimes I just quietly skip my meals the next day, or I'll overdo it a bit with my walking. Just trying to balance the scales, really, so nobody has to worry about me. \n\nDoes... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight when you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... yes, I suppose I am. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly vain or superficial. \n\nIt's just... well, my daughter, and actually a lovely lady from my church, they've both been telling me lately that I look a bit too thin. Which is sweet of them to worry, but... I just don't see it that way at all. If my skirt feels even the slightest bit snug in the morning, or if I step on the scale and it's up just one single pound... I completely panic. It feels as if I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, if that makes any sense? \n\nI just get so frightened of letting myself go. I know I shouldn't let a number on a scale dictate my mood, but I just can't seem to help it. Is... is it normal to worry about it quite that much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look overweight even when others tell you that you're too thin?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I look overweight right now. I'm so sorry if I gave you the wrong impression just now! I know I'm not... you know, heavy. \n\nIt's more that I just really don't see the \"too thin\" part that they seem to see. When I look in the mirror, I don't see a heavy person, but I definitely don't see a frail one either. I just see... well, just me, I suppose. Just an average woman who needs to keep a very close eye on things so they don't slip. I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it properly for you, I just... I really don't see what they see when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is it common for people to struggle with that part?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I'm sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Linda, that's enough now, stop,' but my hands just have a mind of their own and keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nIt's just this one specific area where I seem to lose my grip. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, exactly... you understand it perfectly. It really is just to stop the weight from coming on. 

But it's... oh, it's just so shameful to talk about. I really want to be completely honest with you, though, because I know you're just trying to help and I want to do this properly. Honestly, the secrecy of it all is what finally made me say something and get help. After I eat too much, I've just been... well, quietly going upstairs and making myself throw up. And then I'll just come right back down and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen, or getting ready for bed. I just pretend everything is perfectly fine and normal. 

I hope you don't think I'm a terribly deceitful person for hiding it like that? I really am not usually like this. I just... I didn't want to upset my family, but I know I need to be entirely open with you if we're going to fix it. Am I giving you the right kind of information?

And then I just come back down and act as if I'm just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so nobody notices. \n\nIt feels terribly silly of me to be doing this at my age, and I'm so sorry to even bother you with it. I really hope I'm not overstating things, but... since last winter, I think there has hardly been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... little private eating spells. \n\nAnd then, well, I just try to fix it, you know? Just trying to balance the scales, really, so nobody has to worry about me. \n\nDoes... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight when you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... yes, I suppose I am. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly vain or superficial. \n\nIt's just... well, my daughter, and actually a lovely lady from my church, they've both been telling me lately that I look a bit too thin. Which is sweet of them to worry, but... I just don't see it that way at all. I know I shouldn't let a number on a scale dictate my mood, but I just can't seem to help it. Is... is it normal to worry about it quite that much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look overweight even when others tell you that you're too thin?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I look overweight right now. I'm so sorry if I gave you the wrong impression just now! I know I'm not... you know, heavy. \n\nIt's more that I just really don't see the \"too thin\" part that they seem to see. When I look in the mirror, I don't see a heavy person, but I definitely don't see a frail one either. I just see... well, just me, I suppose. Just an average woman who needs to keep a very close eye on things so they don't slip. I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it properly for you, I just... I really don't see what they see when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I hope that's alright. My weight has actually stayed fairly... well, fairly steady over the past year. \n\nI try very hard to keep it that way, you see. So there haven't been any dramatic jumps up or down, just... maybe a pound or two here and there, which I always try to catch right away before it becomes a problem. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose it can feel a bit difficult to stop sometimes. I really try not to let it get out of hand, I promise I do. \n\nIt's just... once I start, it's almost as if a little switch flips inside me? It's the strangest feeling. It's like I'm just sort of... watching myself keep eating. And there's this part of my brain that's saying, 'Linda, you need to stop now, that's enough,' but... my hands just keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit that. It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is it common for people to struggle with that part?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose that's exactly what I was trying to explain just a moment ago. I just... I really hate to use the phrase 'losing control,' if that's alright? It sounds so... well, so messy. And I really do try to be a very put-together person. \n\nBut yes, it is exactly like that. Like I mentioned, that switch just flips and I become a spectator to my own actions. I'm sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Linda, that's enough now, stop,' but my hands just have a mind of their own and keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nIt's just this one specific area where I seem to lose my grip. I promise I'm completely responsible in every other part of my life! I'm very meticulous at the opticians where I work, and I keep everything else in perfect order. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about not being able to control how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... fearful? No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or fearful, exactly. I hope it's alright that I don't feel that way? \n\nIt's more just... well, a bit of disappointment in myself, I suppose. And then I just come back down and act as if I'm just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so nobody notices. \n\nIt feels terribly silly of me to be doing this at my age, and I'm so sorry to even bother you with it. Is... is this the right sort of thing to be talking to you about?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating too much and then making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose it's been a bit more regular than I'd care to admit. I really hope I'm not overstating things, but... since last winter, I think there has hardly been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... little private eating spells. \n\nAnd then, well, I just try to fix it, you know? To make up for it so my weight doesn't get out of hand. I might make myself sick, like I mentioned, or sometimes I just quietly skip my meals the next day, or I'll overdo it a bit with my walking. Just trying to balance the scales, really, so nobody has to worry about me. \n\nDoes... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight when you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... yes, I suppose I am. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly vain or superficial. \n\nIt's just... well, my daughter, and actually a lovely lady from my church, they've both been telling me lately that I look a bit too thin. Which is sweet of them to worry, but... I just don't see it that way at all. If my skirt feels even the slightest bit snug in the morning, or if I step on the scale and it's up just one single pound... I completely panic. It feels as if I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, if that makes any sense? \n\nI just get so frightened of letting myself go. I know I shouldn't let a number on a scale dictate my mood, but I just can't seem to help it. Is... is it normal to worry about it quite that much?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look overweight even when others tell you that you're too thin?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I look overweight right now. I'm so sorry if I gave you the wrong impression just now! I know I'm not... you know, heavy. \n\nIt's more that I just really don't see the \"too thin\" part that they seem to see. When I look in the mirror, I don't see a heavy person, but I definitely don't see a frail one either. I just see... well, just me, I suppose. Just an average woman who needs to keep a very close eye on things so they don't slip. I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it properly for you, I just... I really don't see what they see when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I hope that's alright. My weight has actually stayed fairly... well, fairly steady over the past year. \n\nI try very hard to keep it that way, you see. So there haven't been any dramatic jumps up or down, just... maybe a pound or two here and there, which I always try to catch right away before it becomes a problem. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose it can feel a bit difficult to stop sometimes. I really try not to let it get out of hand, I promise I do. \n\nIt's just... once I start, it's almost as if a little switch flips inside me? It's the strangest feeling. It's like I'm just sort of... watching myself keep eating. And there's this part of my brain that's saying, 'Linda, you need to stop now, that's enough,' but... my hands just keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit that. It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is it common for people to struggle with that part?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose that's exactly what I was trying to explain just a moment ago. I just... I really hate to use the phrase 'losing control,' if that's alright? It sounds so... well, so messy. And I really do try to be a very put-together person. \n\nBut yes, it is exactly like that. Like I mentioned, that switch just flips and I become a spectator to my own actions. I'm sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Linda, that's enough now, stop,' but my hands just have a mind of their own and keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nIt's just this one specific area where I seem to lose my grip. I promise I'm completely responsible in every other part of my life! I'm very meticulous at the opticians where I work, and I keep everything else in perfect order. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about not being able to control how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... fearful? No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or fearful, exactly. I hope it's alright that I don't feel that way? \n\nIt's more just... well, a bit of disappointment in myself, I suppose. But certainly not fear or panic. I try very hard not to be an anxious person, you see. I like to think I handle things rather calmly, both at the practice and at home. So when I think about those little slip-ups, I don't sit and worry about them. I just tell myself that tomorrow is a new day and I'll be much better next time. \n\nI really do try to just... stay positive and move forward, rather than getting worked up. I hope that makes sense? Am I answering your questions properly?\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh... oh dear. Well... I suppose I do... um... sort of try to undo it, sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for that. I really am quite embarrassed to even say it out loud. \n\nIt's just... well, since last winter, I suppose there hasn't really been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... private little spells. And then, well, I just feel so dreadfully guilty, so I try to make up for it. Sometimes I might make myself sick, or I'll just skip my meals the next day, or I'll go for very, very long walks to try and balance it all out. \n\nI don't do it to be bad, I promise! I just want to fix the mistake I made. I try to keep it to maybe just once or twice a week, no more than three times... I really try not to let it get out of hand. Is it... is it terribly awful that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm being completely honest with you, even if it's not very flattering.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, I'm so sorry, did I not explain that properly just a moment ago? I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need... I know I can get a bit flustered and talk around things when I'm embarrassed. \n\nBut yes, just like I was saying... since last winter, there has hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those private eating spells. And then, because I feel so awful about it, I try to make up for it. So I'll make myself throw up, or I'll skip my meals entirely the next day, or I'll just really overdo my walking to burn it off. \n\nI hope that's a bit clearer for your notes? Does that answer your question better this time? I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or avoid it, I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you make yourself throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, I... I really want to be helpful, I hope I'm not being frustrating by repeating myself. I know you need a specific number for your records. \n\nIf I have to count it by the month... well, like I mentioned, since last winter there really hasn't been a week that goes by without at least one of those... those episodes, and then me trying to fix it by throwing up, or fasting, or walking for hours. \n\nSo, if it's one to three times a week, I suppose that would be... maybe four to twelve times a month? I try to keep it on the lower end, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to stay presentable and neat, and I worry so much about losing control of that. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it properly for you, I just... I really don't see what they see when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I hope that's alright. My weight has actually stayed fairly... well, fairly steady over the past year. \n\nI try very hard to keep it that way, you see. So there haven't been any dramatic jumps up or down, just... maybe a pound or two here and there, which I always try to catch right away before it becomes a problem. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose it can feel a bit difficult to stop sometimes. I really try not to let it get out of hand, I promise I do. \n\nIt's just... once I start, it's almost as if a little switch flips inside me? It's the strangest feeling. It's like I'm just sort of... watching myself keep eating. And there's this part of my brain that's saying, 'Linda, you need to stop now, that's enough,' but... my hands just keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit that. It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is it common for people to struggle with that part?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose that's exactly what I was trying to explain just a moment ago. I just... I really hate to use the phrase 'losing control,' if that's alright? It sounds so... well, so messy. And I really do try to be a very put-together person. \n\nBut yes, it is exactly like that. Like I mentioned, that switch just flips and I become a spectator to my own actions. I'm sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Linda, that's enough now, stop,' but my hands just have a mind of their own and keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nIt's just this one specific area where I seem to lose my grip. I promise I'm completely responsible in every other part of my life! I'm very meticulous at the opticians where I work, and I keep everything else in perfect order. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about not being able to control how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... fearful? No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or fearful, exactly. I hope it's alright that I don't feel that way? \n\nIt's more just... well, a bit of disappointment in myself, I suppose. But certainly not fear or panic. I try very hard not to be an anxious person, you see. I like to think I handle things rather calmly, both at the practice and at home. So when I think about those little slip-ups, I don't sit and worry about them. I just tell myself that tomorrow is a new day and I'll be much better next time. \n\nI really do try to just... stay positive and move forward, rather than getting worked up. I hope that makes sense? Am I answering your questions properly?\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh... oh dear. Well... I suppose I do... um... sort of try to undo it, sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for that. I really am quite embarrassed to even say it out loud. \n\nIt's just... well, since last winter, I suppose there hasn't really been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... private little spells. And then, well, I just feel so dreadfully guilty, so I try to make up for it. Sometimes I might make myself sick, or I'll just skip my meals the next day, or I'll go for very, very long walks to try and balance it all out. \n\nI don't do it to be bad, I promise! I just want to fix the mistake I made. I try to keep it to maybe just once or twice a week, no more than three times... I really try not to let it get out of hand. Is it... is it terribly awful that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm being completely honest with you, even if it's not very flattering.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, I'm so sorry, did I not explain that properly just a moment ago? I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need... I know I can get a bit flustered and talk around things when I'm embarrassed. \n\nBut yes, just like I was saying... since last winter, there has hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those private eating spells. And then, because I feel so awful about it, I try to make up for it. So I'll make myself throw up, or I'll skip my meals entirely the next day, or I'll just really overdo my walking to burn it off. \n\nI hope that's a bit clearer for your notes? Does that answer your question better this time? I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or avoid it, I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you make yourself throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, I... I really want to be helpful, I hope I'm not being frustrating by repeating myself. I know you need a specific number for your records. \n\nIf I have to count it by the month... well, like I mentioned, since last winter there really hasn't been a week that goes by without at least one of those... those episodes, and then me trying to fix it by throwing up, or fasting, or walking for hours. \n\nSo, if it's one to three times a week, I suppose that would be... maybe four to twelve times a month? I try to keep it on the lower end, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I'm so sorry if my previous answers weren't precise enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to maintain a certain weight or body shape when you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, yes, I suppose that has a lot to do with it. I do hope you don't think I'm just being terribly vain, especially at my age. I know I shouldn't fuss so much over my figure. \n\nIt's just... well, my daughter, and actually a very sweet woman from my church, they've both mentioned recently that they think I look too thin. Which is kind of them to care, I suppose, but... oh, it's so embarrassing to admit this out loud... I just pretend everything is perfectly fine and normal. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm a terribly deceitful person for hiding it like that? I really am not usually like this. I just... Oh, goodness... well, I want to be as accurate as possible for you. I'm not entirely certain if it's always exactly twice a week, every single week. I suppose that's exactly what I was trying to explain just a moment ago. I just... I really hate to use the phrase 'losing control,' if that's alright? It sounds so... well, so messy. And I really do try to be a very put-together person. \n\nBut yes, it is exactly like that. Like I mentioned, that switch just flips and I become a spectator to my own actions. I'm sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Linda, that's enough now, stop,' but my hands just have a mind of their own and keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nIt's just this one specific area where I seem to lose my grip. I promise I'm completely responsible in every other part of my life! I'm very meticulous at the opticians where I work, and I keep everything else in perfect order. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about not being able to control how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... fearful? No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or fearful, exactly. I hope it's alright that I don't feel that way? \n\nIt's more just... well, a bit of disappointment in myself, I suppose. But certainly not fear or panic. I try very hard not to be an anxious person, you see. I like to think I handle things rather calmly, both at the practice and at home. So when I think about those little slip-ups, I don't sit and worry about them. I just tell myself that tomorrow is a new day and I'll be much better next time. \n\nI really do try to just... stay positive and move forward, rather than getting worked up. I hope that makes sense? Am I answering your questions properly?\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh... oh dear. Well... I suppose I do... um... sort of try to undo it, sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for that. I really am quite embarrassed to even say it out loud. \n\nIt's just... well, since last winter, I suppose there hasn't really been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... private little spells. And then, well, I just feel so dreadfully guilty, so I try to make up for it. Sometimes I might make myself sick, or I'll just skip my meals the next day, or I'll go for very, very long walks to try and balance it all out. \n\nI don't do it to be bad, I promise! I just want to fix the mistake I made. I try to keep it to maybe just once or twice a week, no more than three times... I really try not to let it get out of hand. I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need... Which is kind of them to care, I suppose, but... oh, it's so embarrassing to admit this out loud... So there haven't been any dramatic jumps up or down, just... maybe a pound or two here and there, which I always try to catch right away before it becomes a problem. I really do try my best to be careful so it doesn't fluctuate too much. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a good thing? Did I answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose it can feel a bit difficult to stop sometimes. I really try not to let it get out of hand, I promise I do. \n\nIt's just... once I start, it's almost as if a little switch flips inside me? It's the strangest feeling. It's like I'm just sort of... watching myself keep eating. And there's this part of my brain that's saying, 'Linda, you need to stop now, that's enough,' but... my hands just keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to admit that. It sounds so silly, a woman my age not being able to just put a fork down. I do try to be good most of the time, I really do. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is it common for people to struggle with that part?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose that's exactly what I was trying to explain just a moment ago. I just... I really hate to use the phrase 'losing control,' if that's alright? It sounds so... well, so messy. And I really do try to be a very put-together person. \n\nBut yes, it is exactly like that. Like I mentioned, that switch just flips and I become a spectator to my own actions. I'm sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, 'Linda, that's enough now, stop,' but my hands just have a mind of their own and keep reaching for more anyway. \n\nIt's just this one specific area where I seem to lose my grip. I promise I'm completely responsible in every other part of my life! I'm very meticulous at the opticians where I work, and I keep everything else in perfect order. It's just... just this one silly thing where I slip up. \n\nI hope it's okay that I put it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you think about not being able to control how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... fearful? No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or fearful, exactly. I hope it's alright that I don't feel that way? \n\nIt's more just... well, a bit of disappointment in myself, I suppose. But certainly not fear or panic. I try very hard not to be an anxious person, you see. I like to think I handle things rather calmly, both at the practice and at home. So when I think about those little slip-ups, I don't sit and worry about them. I just tell myself that tomorrow is a new day and I'll be much better next time. \n\nI really do try to just... stay positive and move forward, rather than getting worked up. I hope that makes sense? Am I answering your questions properly?\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh... oh dear. Well... I suppose I do... um... sort of try to undo it, sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for that. I really am quite embarrassed to even say it out loud. \n\nIt's just... well, since last winter, I suppose there hasn't really been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... private little spells. And then, well, I just feel so dreadfully guilty, so I try to make up for it. Sometimes I might make myself sick, or I'll just skip my meals the next day, or I'll go for very, very long walks to try and balance it all out. \n\nI don't do it to be bad, I promise! I just want to fix the mistake I made. I try to keep it to maybe just once or twice a week, no more than three times... I really try not to let it get out of hand. Is it... is it terribly awful that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm being completely honest with you, even if it's not very flattering.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, I'm so sorry, did I not explain that properly just a moment ago? I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need... I know I can get a bit flustered and talk around things when I'm embarrassed. \n\nBut yes, just like I was saying... since last winter, there has hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those private eating spells. And then, because I feel so awful about it, I try to make up for it. So I'll make myself throw up, or I'll skip my meals entirely the next day, or I'll just really overdo my walking to burn it off. \n\nI hope that's a bit clearer for your notes? Does that answer your question better this time? I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or avoid it, I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you make yourself throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, I... I really want to be helpful, I hope I'm not being frustrating by repeating myself. I know you need a specific number for your records. \n\nIf I have to count it by the month... well, like I mentioned, since last winter there really hasn't been a week that goes by without at least one of those... those episodes, and then me trying to fix it by throwing up, or fasting, or walking for hours. \n\nSo, if it's one to three times a week, I suppose that would be... maybe four to twelve times a month? I try to keep it on the lower end, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I'm so sorry if my previous answers weren't precise enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to maintain a certain weight or body shape when you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, yes, I suppose that has a lot to do with it. I do hope you don't think I'm just being terribly vain, especially at my age. I know I shouldn't fuss so much over my figure. \n\nIt's just... well, my daughter, and actually a very sweet woman from my church, they've both mentioned recently that they think I look too thin. Which is kind of them to care, I suppose, but... oh, it's so embarrassing to admit this out loud... I still just completely panic if my skirt feels even a tiny bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one little pound. I get this awful feeling as if I've somehow let myself become absolutely huge overnight. \n\nI just want to keep everything neat and under control, you know? I really try not to be silly about it. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too terribly foolish to you.\n\nDoctor: Are you making yourself throw up primarily to prevent weight gain?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly... you understand it perfectly. It really is just to stop the weight from coming on. \n\nBut it's... oh, it's just so shameful to talk about. I really want to be completely honest with you, though, because I know you're just trying to help and I want to do this properly. Honestly, the secrecy of it all is what finally made me say something and get help. After I eat too much, I've just been... well, quietly going upstairs and making myself throw up. And then I'll just come right back down and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen, or getting ready for bed. I just pretend everything is perfectly fine and normal. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm a terribly deceitful person for hiding it like that? I really am not usually like this. I just... I didn't want to upset my family, but I know I need to be entirely open with you if we're going to fix it. Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat too much without then trying to make up for it by throwing up, fasting, or exercising?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no, I don't think so. I really hope that's not a terrible thing to admit. \n\nWhenever I have one of those... those moments where I just eat too much, I always feel like I have to fix it right away. If I don't, I just feel so dreadfully guilty and out of control. So I always make sure to do something about it, whether it's the throwing up, or just not eating the next day, or taking a very, very long walk. \n\nI've never really just... let it be. I hope that's the right answer? I know it probably sounds a bit obsessive, and I really don't want you to think I'm completely unhinged. I just... I always try to make up for it. Does that help with what you're trying to figure out?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat too much without then trying to make up for it by throwing up, fasting, or exercising, but instead just continue with your normal activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I don't believe so. I think we just talked about that a moment ago, didn't we? I hope I wasn't unclear before. \n\nI really never do just... go back to normal activities after I eat like that. I always, always have to do something to fix it, like throwing up or skipping meals. I just couldn't bear to let it sit there, you know? Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up, fast, or exercise excessively to compensate for overeating at least twice a week?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I want to be as accurate as possible for you. I'm not entirely certain if it's always exactly twice a week, every single week. But, um... to be completely honest, since last winter, there has hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. And then, of course, I try to make up for it by throwing up, or skipping my meals, or really overdoing my walking. \n\nI know that probably sounds quite bad, letting it go on since last winter. I'm so embarrassed to admit it's been happening that regularly, and I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for it. I do try my best to keep it under control. Does that answer your question the way you need me to? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you typically eat an amount of food that would be considered large by most people during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I suppose it depends on what someone might consider large? Definitely far more than I would ever put on my plate for a proper meal. I know that sounds awful, and I'm so sorry if that's shocking to hear. I really do try to be good most of the time. Is that... does that count as a large amount for what you're asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have one of these episodes where you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, I think... well, I might have just mentioned this a moment ago, but I want to be absolutely sure I'm giving you the right details. \n\nUm... like I was saying, it's not always a set number every single week, but... since last winter, there really hasn't been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells.
